In grade 8, on the second last day of school, I brought a magnifying glass to school and me and my friends were writing our names on pieces of paper in the back parking lot where we had recess. We were hiding in this alcove where the garbage dumpsters were located so that no teacher would see. Well, just my luck, my paper caught fire so I put it in the corner and put a bunch of garbage like paper bags and cardboard on top to try and like stifle it. Well, I was grade 8 and didnt really get things like fire. I left feeling like I escaped getting in trouble. I turn around and theres this mini inferno behind me. Of, you think who cares were in the alcove where no one can see us. Unluckily however, a janitor promptly came out the door carrying two large bags of garbage. You can probably imagine how the rest turned out. In the end, I was suspended for 5 days. Who cares, only one day of school left till high school.

96-4 is my favourite level. The first time I completed it, I looked at the lowest hiscore and saw how they avoided all the drones. #246253 6 2006-10-27T00:41:02Z Hendor
If it is 19-4 you are wondering about, the easiest way to do it is jump on the launchpads in the corner and go up to the top switches and jump across and get them and fall through the bottom gap and land on top of the lower switch's house. Then just fall through and get the bottom one. The finish switch is pretty easy. If you want a map I'll make one.

#253168 6 2006-11-11T21:25:18Z Hendor
Getting out of the far left rooms is kinda tricky.
Don't think this has been mentioned, but the idea is to jump half a second early so that you pass the floorguard when it is still moving left when you pass over it. That way you get a bit of extra time to run right. Then, jump, as soon as you leave the tunnel, so it does not catch up. Hope that helps.

#259994 17 2006-11-26T18:53:20Z Hendor
I searched for a map but didnt find one, so I made one for you. My route here doesn't get too much gold, I finish in just under 90, so I can make another one if you want that gets more gold.



The numbers show the relatively tricky spots:

1 - I just go down the chute a bit so that there is no way I could get shot. Theres plenty of time to come back up before the thwump gets you.

2 - When you get to the top of the chute, wait at the dot for the chaingun in the first room to be directly across from you on its way down the left side of the room. Then, you can just fall straight onto where I show and continue the route.

3 - Here, you want to make sure the gauss in the second to last room does not shoot you when you go up to get the exit switch. You can do that by jumping off the far left wall, out of the gauss' line of sight. Then just get the switch and fall down to the exit following the path I show, so that the left gauss at the bottom doesnt kill you.

Good Luck

#268390 2 2006-12-11T22:33:02Z Hendor
Hi, Welcome to the new and improved N City. Thanks to Louie50 for the idea.

My goal is to get as many people as possible on the City, so don't wait, request yours now, just be sure to follow the format I have shown below.

---How I'm Doing It---

I use N Poser to make the ninjas and mostly Paint to put it together.
I will update the City whenever a few people have requested and I will keep a list of who is in each city just above the actual city. If you are looking for a certain person, just use the Ctrl-F search function and enter their name.

---How you can get on the City---

To get on the city, just make a post that says:

1) What color you want your ninja
- Any color

2) What action you want your ninja to be doing
-This can be pretty much anything

3) What city you want to be in and where in it
-You can be on any surface you want as long as you are connected to the outside air (no bubbles).

4) What name you want
-I will take the name right out of your sig image if you have one so that it is easily recognizable.

---Waiting List---

-EMPTY-

#269778 8 2006-12-14T02:27:35Z Hendor
Quote: Viridovix, December 13, 2006 09:17 pm
I'm sure there's tons of freeware out there that people use. And what's fbf? All I've heard of is .sol hacking, cheat engine, and Speed Hack.

fbf stands for frame-by-frame and it is when the game progresses one frame at a time, allowing much better control and precision. A frame is 0.025 seconds in the game and you can play frame by frame in Ned when you don't have caps lock on.

try it if you havnt

#285284 17 2007-01-13T21:07:40Z Hendor
Quote: zapkt, January 13, 2007 03:38 pm
It's stupid, I would have a better ratio with less 0th...
There is no "better score." This whole concept is simply a measure of what proportion of your scores are 0ths. The lower your score, the larger the percent of your scores are that are 0ths.

I personally think that a far better calculation would be:

(# of 0ths / # of scores) x 100%

This would give you a more understandable number. For me, this would be (1/250) = 0.4%. All this says is that 0.4% of my scores are 0ths. This is why I was saying cyberjuda's was going to be the best. 16 of his 42 scores are 0ths (38%).

#295429 27 2007-02-09T13:31:28Z Hendor
When someone has a quote with a name and date of whoever said it, its because they clicked the QUOTE button in the upper right hand corner of every single post. When you click that, it opens a replying window, much like the one I'm using right now except below where you write your message, it has a quote box, where everything in the post you are quoting is copied. You can then edit out parts of the quoted post you are not going to be replying to.

Or, you can just type : [QUOTE= ] with details after the = sign. I never really use that.

#296814 2 2007-02-13T04:07:39Z Hendor
Really dificult to make a map for this, so I made a demo. It is attached. Pretty easy to do. Just take the jumps up the left hand side of the pit slowly. You can modify how you get down the right side however you want. I just thought this was pretty easy.

To view the demo, open the attached file. Right click, Select All and then Copy it. Paste all of this into the box on the left hand side of the Userlevels menu after you have selected Userlevels from the main menu of N. Then simply press Watch Replay which is located under this box and the demo will start playing. Sorry if you didn't need that much instruction.

#299802 17 2007-02-22T04:56:55Z Hendor
To get the renders from Planet Renders, become a member (takes 30 seconds). Then, find a render you like when browsing the gallery. Click on it, and it should pop up in a new window in the full resolution (it won't do that if you're not a member.) Then right-click and Save As somewhere one your computer. Make sure you save it as a .PNG. Then you can open it in Photoshop or GIMP or whatever you want. Just put it on top of your image and resize it however you want.

Hope that helps!

#301763 20 2007-02-28T03:18:21Z Hendor
Quote: Zhaine, 2007-02-28T00:55:28Z
To me, and again I know this is highly unscientific, it's just blatantly impossible.

Thank you Zhaine! That's precisely what I was trying to say.

I have thought of another argument that helps my case. Here are the original 10 lines from NHigh I showed you plus four more from just before them:

105. L - 117 frames
222. Nothing - 2 frames
224. R - 9 frames
233. L - 2 frames
235. R - 1 frame
236. Nothing - 1 frame
237. L - 1 frame
238. R - 1 frame
239. Nothing - 1 frame
240. L - 1 frame
241. Nothing - 1 frame
242. L - 1 frame
243. LR - 1 frame
244. L - 1 frame

So, looking at the beginning of that, Kryx had run across the top of the map just holding L for 117 frames. That makes sense. He then falls off the edge and straightens out his fall over the next 13 frames. That too makes sense. Then, once he is falling straight, he hits that flurry of keys. Why? He is falling straight down. The only explanation is that he was using fbf and before each click he had to do something. So, just to keep his fall straight, he hit left, then right, then nothing.

Now, for contrast, here is the demo analysis for Toad, who place 1st on the same level:

095. L - 139 frames
234. Nothing - 2 frames
236. R - 24 frames
260. Nothing - 39 frames
299. R - 52 frames

Yes, those are all the keys he pressed to go from the top right to the bottom left. Speaks for itself.

Basically to sum this up: If Kryx had the ability to hit keys that ridiculously quickly, what possible explanation is there for him using it in such a pointless place, except that he used fbf. I hope spect is convinced now.

EDIT - just got 5th on the level

#302797 3 2007-03-05T22:59:20Z Hendor
I would recommend Attilas too. Other than that, here are a couple "moves" that people frequently use. I've also put a level where the top highscore incorporates it in brackets:

Perpendicular jump (see 88-1): This is when the ninja runs up a slope and then springs up much higher than you can normally jump. Heres how you do it: Let's say you're running right and there is a slope ahead. You hold right and run up onto the slope. Right at the instant you want to jump, release right and hit jump. There's this little glitch that sometimes causes you to skid instead of jumping after you release right, but after some practise, you can avoid this most times.

Corner Jump (see 90-1): Well, not much to this. Just skim the corner and press jump. There is only about 1 frame where pressing jump will actually make you do a big corner jump, so these are still a large part luck, even for the best of players.

Large Jumps off of Bounce Blocks (see 10-2): These jumps are actually two jumps. They require you to land on the bounce block following a fairly big fall. It will sag under him and will then pop back up. The goal is to jump right when the ninja gets near the very bottom of the "sag", and then once again as soon as possible after. These can be done vertically(off the top) and horizontally(off the side). They are actually relatively easy to do and most highscorers can do them 99% of the time given a large enough fall before hitting the bounce block.

If you think of any others I can explain them, but I think thats good for now.

#307131 5 2007-03-23T02:59:31Z Hendor
Works for Canada too!

Here's how it works step by step if anyone cares. The instructions are in bold:

let x be the first three numbers of your number and y be the last four.

x
x 80
80x
Add 1
80x + 1
x 250
20 000x + 250
Add last 4 digits
20 000x + 250 + y
Add last 4 digits again
20 000x + 250 + 2y
Subtract 250
20 000x + 2y
Divide by 2
10 000x + y

Ta da!!

Sub your first three digits in for x and last four for y into that final answer.

#308203 1076 2007-03-27T22:45:14Z Hendor
As far as I know, there are three ways of viewing highscores:

1 - In the game in the highscores menu that you select from the main menu. Just click an episode and a level and wait for the list to dowload. Then click a person's name and you c an watch their demo.

2 - NHigh. This is jg9000's program. It is an external program that downloads the scores from the metanet servers. Besides seeing the rankings for the levels, you can view many neat reports, like how many scores a certain person has, or how many 0ths a person has. Very useful. Find it here.

3 - The metanet site. Not really very useful. I never really use it except when I'm at a computer that doesn't have N or NHigh. Find them here

Hope I've been helpful.

#316265 3 2007-04-27T20:44:42Z Hendor
Next time you have a problem, try asking it in the N Help subforum which was made for these kind of requests. This High Scores forum is where we post about the highscores for certain levels from the game.

Anyways, you couldn't have known that, so here is a map with two routes. The red route requires a double jump off of the bounce block and the green route requires you to jump of the side of one of the bounceblocks on the way up.

Oh, if you don't know how to do a "double jump", I posted how in this thread. Here's the excerpt:

Quote
Large Jumps off of Bounce Blocks (see level 10-2): These jumps are actually two jumps. They require you to land on the bounce block following a fairly big fall. It will sag under you and will then pop back up. The goal is to jump right when the ninja gets near the very bottom of the "sag", and then once again as soon as possible after. You should shoot upwards quite quickly as you have actually jumped twice, one of those times off of a bounceblock that was moving up quickly. These can be done vertically(off the top) and horizontally(off the side). They are actually relatively easy to do and most highscorers can do them 99% of the time given a large enough fall before hitting the bounce block.

Good Luck!

Tweak UI is a program released by Microsoft along with Windows 95 I believe. It is in existance so people can mess with the embedded system settings without going into the registry and potentially screwing things up big time. They have been updating it with new stuff as the years went on. To get rid of the Recycle Bin, just downolad and install it and uncheck the box beside Recycle Bin on the Desktop tab.

#322310 3 2007-05-18T22:26:19Z Hendor
Yes, it would happen to us if we finished 12-2 with 0.35 seconds left. The idea when you are playing episodes is that the amount of time carries on from level to level. So, if you finished 12-2 with 87 seconds to go, you start 12-3 with 87 seconds. You only get 90 seconds at the beginning of each level, in your case, 12-0. To beat episode 12, you are going to have to restart the episode and either beat each level faster than you did before and/or get more pieces of gold, because they add 2 seconds to your total time.

#326853 2 2007-06-03T03:35:57Z Hendor
Whenever I play a level and get a highscore on it, I must exit the level and press Submit for it to appear on the boards.

Whenever I play an episode and score it, N will automatically submit it and it will appear on the boards. It should tell you this upon completion of the episode in a little message box that says something like "New Online Highscore... Submitting..." In addition, if you achieve a level score while in the process of playing an episode, it will be automatically submitted in the same fashion as above.

I hope I have been helpful, good day.

#330720 4 2007-06-13T03:03:43Z Hendor
Quote: Azjps, June 12, 2007 07:43 pm
By the way, while I'm here, is there anyway to adjust the size of an image (outside of directly changing the image)?

You could be either meaning the size as in the dimensions or as in the storage size (number of kB). For the first, if you're using paint, just go to Image>Stretch/Skew. If you mean the storage size, just save it as a .jpg or .gif rather than a .png. While it will look worse, quality doesn't really matter for a route map

Let's assume that the money tax free. Any bank that you're prepared to give $1,000,000 to would probably offer a fairly significant interest rate. Let's conservatively assume 6 or 7 percent. That would give you $60 000 to $70 000 every year keeping that same million dollars in the bank untouched. Sounds pretty good to me.

#342233 10 2007-07-12T21:29:19Z Hendor
I'm starting University this September in Kinesiology. At the moment, I think I am most interested in medicine. Tons of reasons for that: The money, the travel oppurtunities, the satisfaction of helping people... There was a pre-med course at my University, but I didn't have the 92% average to get into it. I had a measly 86%.

About high school courses... It does matter a bit in my opinion. In my last year, I took absolutely everything that a scientific university course could possibly require. My load was Physics, Bio, Chem, Calculus, Discrete Geometry, English and Kinesiology. My university course only required one math course and one of Bio or Chem. While my average could have been better if I took easy courses like economics or law, if I hadn't taken all the sciences that I did, I would have closed a lot of doors. For example, I'm thinking about minoring in Astrophysics. I would never be able to do this had I not taken both Calculus and Physics.

Of course, things in the States might be very different than up here in the Arctic. We don't take SATs. Most important thing really is to make sure you actually get into university/college. Don't get obsessed with taking everything, blowing up and not having the grades to be accepted.

Good Luck.


PS - As far as money goes, I lucky out hugely. My dad is on faculty at the University. I save $330 per course I take. As I'm taking 10 courses this year, I save over $3000 on my tuition. Also, I have a $1000 scholarship for entering with an average over 85. All told, I have to pay the University just over $1000 dollars, which covers both tuition and all other fees.

#16503 1 2007-08-01T03:40:18Z Hendor


Hi! Made these today.

They are all 128x128 px so they look great either on a desktop or in an Rocket/Object Dock. The .ico files for all four colors are hosted here in a single zip file. I also tossed in the .psd file if you wanna take a look.

How to Use

1. Download the zipped folder to your desktop (or wherever you want actually.)
2. Open it and drag the icon(s) you want to the desktop.
3. Right click on the icon to N and select Properties.
4. Click on the tab in this window that says Shortcut.
5. Click on Change Icon button and select the icon that you previously dragged onto the desktop.
6. Click Apply and you're done. Place on an Object Dock for best effect.

The .psd file contains all 4 colors on different layers along with 6 other layers that make up the rest of the icon. They're also 512px for extra-goodiness. If you want to know how I made them, ask, and I'll post some instructions. Thanks.

Found another little innovation that counters having to wait for the leftmost drone at the end. If you can pull the start bit off fast enough, the right drone hasn't moved as far so it is able to see you in that little gap, otherwise it sees you later on. This means that when you get the doorswitch you can immediately drop down and continue, being a good 0.5 seconds faster than having to wait for the drone to stop shooting, since it has seen you earlier, and is also further away when you do the big jump.

#366258 308 2007-09-20T04:15:35Z Hendor
Quote: usaswim, September 09, 2007 11:40 pm
Amino acids make up DNA

Sorry to tell you now... but that's completely wrong. DNA is made up of nucleotide monomers. Each nucleotide is comprised of 1 sugar, 1 phosphate base and 1 nitrogenous base. I think what was confusing you was the nitrogenous base part. This will be one of Adenosine, Cytosine, Guanine, Thymine or Urasil. While they sound like it, these are not amino acids.

As for all of your recent work, it's all quite beautiful. I'm sure your teachers have been quite... surprised.

#369128 5 2007-10-02T19:33:59Z Hendor
I read a biography of Einstien over the summer and it did a really good job of explaining relativity and its effects. I'll try and help you picture the concept.

Before Einstein, space was considered constant, a medium in which all motions took place. The shortest distance between two objects is a straight line. Einstein proposed that space is highly variable, that the shortest distance between two objects is not always a straight line. This required the belief that space was four-dimensional, not three-dimensional, as previously thought. This whole concept is extraordinarily hard to picture, so we must use a metaphor: You may have heard of the sheet and ball model of gravity. Basically, it says that space can be imagined as a sheet, stretched perfectly flat. Something that can produce gravitational forces, such as a planet, can be imagined as a ball resting on the sheet. Naturally, this ball will create an indentation in the sheet, such that the area around the ball will slope down, towards the bottom of the ball. If there are two balls near each other, they will "roll" together and touch. If you were to roll one ball so that it passes near another larger ball, it will curve, and come to rest lying against that ball.

Look at the image below. The orange circle is the sun, the smaller blue one is the earth and the much smaller white dot is a star. The line shows the light emitted by the star's path. You can see it curves a bit and makes it appear that the star was actually where the blue dot is.



Thinking about this idea, Einstein used his general relativity field equations and calculated that light must bend a certain amount when it passes the Sun. Seven years later, during a solar eclipse, he was proved correct.

#17653 1 2007-10-24T04:39:21Z Hendor
Thanks for reading this before you answer. A couple weeks ago I randomly tried holding my breath while sitting at the computer. I got up to about 1:30. I thought there must be special techniques that let people get to 9 minutes. There are, and since then, I've now hit 3 mintues. If you want to try it my way, follow these instructions:

Of course there are risks associated with doing this, so, um, please don't kill yourself.

1 - For a minute or so, breath slowly in the nose and out the mouth.
2 - Then, take 7 or 8 really deep, really quick breaths through the mouth. This is called hyperventilating.
3 - You'll likely be slightly lightheaded, so wait just a couple seconds until this passes.
4 - Sit up straight and take the deepest breath possible. Start your watch or timer.
5 - Hold the breath and relax your body. Try not to think about much. Once you feel you've gone as far as you can go, try exhaling nearly all the way. I find I can hold it about 20 seconds more after I've done this. If you've gone as far as you can safely go, your legs should tingle when you finally inhale.
6 - Stop the watch or timer and place your vote.

The Science Behind holding your breath

Interestingly, after holding your breath, the reason we are forced to breathe is not the body's need for oxygen to power muscles, but the need for oxygen to increase the pH in your blood. (If you don't already know this, oxygen causes your blood pH to rise (become basic), while carbon dioxide causes your blood pH to drop (become acidic.)) As carbon dioxide levels build up, the pH drops. Oxygen is needed to correct this, so your brain makes makes you inhale. So, now your saying, if only we could find a way to start with the pH higher. Then, as carbon dioxide builds up, the pH is first brought back down to normal levels before it starts becoming acidic. Logically, we could probably double our total time. This is precisely what hyperventilating is and as you have seen, I saw nearly a two-fold increase. Lastly, my idea of exhaling towards the end of your trial was built on this idea. I figured that exhaling the carbon dioxide in your lungs would raise the pH slightly. I'm guessing that getting rid of this carbon dioxide outweighs losing whatever oxygen is leftover in your lungs.

Cool stuff.

Quote: Site's FAQ
FreeRice has a custom database containing thousands of words at varying degrees of difficulty. There are words appropriate for people just learning English and words that will challenge the most scholarly professors. In between are thousands of words for students, business people, homemakers, doctors, truck drivers, retired people everyone!

FreeRice automatically adjusts to your level of vocabulary. It starts by giving you words at different levels of difficulty and then, based on how you do, assigns you an approximate starting level. You then determine a more exact level for yourself as you play. When you get a word wrong, you go to an easier level. When you get three words in a row right, you go to a harder level. This one-to-three ratio is best for keeping you at the outer fringe of your vocabulary, where learning can take place.

There are 50 levels in all, but it is rare for people to get above level 48.

#17706 1 2007-10-28T23:50:29Z Hendor
Hey! Today I'll show you how to create cool looking metallic shapes using nothing but blending options. See the end of this post for a couple examples if you want.

:: STEP ONE ::

Open a new Photoshop document of any size with a white background. Open a new layer and select the Custom Shape tool which is found here:


:: STEP TWO ::

Choose a shape you want from the menu at the top. Make sure it has large areas of pure black. For my examples, I'll use a hand. So, draw the hand somewhere in the middle of your canvas as shown and Rasterize the layer by right-clicking on the layer in the layer menu and selecting Rasterize:



:: STEP THREE ::

Open up Blending Options by double-clicking on the layer in the layers window. To start, we want to add a drop shadow, an inner glow, a bevel and a gradient overlay. Apply the following changes:

Drop Shadow

Opacity = 100%
Distance = 0 px
Size = 12 px

Inner Glow

Color = Pure White
Choke = 10
Size = 10

Bevel and Emboss

Gloss Contour = Ring

Gradient Overlay

Opacity = 60%
Gradient = Black to White (should be black at the bottom and white at the top)

Your final product should look like this:



:: STEP FOUR ::

Open a new layer, choose a new custom shape and put it in the middle of the canvas again. Rasterize it. You can click the eye beside the previous layer to make it invisible for the time being. Right click that first layer that already has the layer effects and click Copy Layer Style. This copies all of the effects you added before. Then, right click on the new layer with the new object and click Paste Layer Style. Ta da!

As far as this tutorial goes, you're done. You can add lots of other things including color overlays, pattern overlays and reflections. If you have any problems, ask away.

GIMP is the GNU Image Manipulation Program. It was created for people who dont have/can't afford Adobe Photohop, the industry standard. You can do nearly everything that you can do in Photoshop in GIMP. You can find the actual progam here. #377612 2 2007-11-08T02:10:23Z Hendor
I've just played a couple levels, but I've taken a look at most of them. Nearly every single level has too many objects where they are not needed at all. You guys do this with three items: mines, launchpads and doors. Sure the latter two look cool and may be kinda fun to go flying through, they greatly increase the loading time of the level, they make the level look messy and frankly noobish and it makes the level not a lot of fun for more experienced players. So, in the levels where you have whole columns and rows full of launchpads (Mars Dungeon 2 is the worst case), take them ALL out. Same goes for the repeating pattern of {door switch - door - door switch - door}. In the above mentioned level however, taking them all out would make for an excrutiatingly boring level. You want to make sure all parts of your level have a point.

To sum up what you need to do: WAYY less objects, more simplicity(no spiraling tunnels). Minimalistic is often better than complex.

#386429 17 2007-12-05T02:31:15Z Hendor
Starting a level or episode with less time would make no difference at all to highscorers. If you check the boards, there are very, very few episodes that end with scores under 200 seconds. If you were to start with 45 or 60 seconds left as people have been saying, it still wouldn't be any harder than it was originally.

What would be a good idea is to make an option to allow time to pass faster. You could set time to pass x times faster. That way, even if you got the total gold count up to 300 seconds after the first level of an episode, it would take far less time to come down. You could set some of the harder episodes to just 1.5x or 2x, while some of the really easy episodes to 3, 4 or even 5x. You could even have competitions for each episode to see who could beat it at the highest time factor (This isn't the same as the kind of episode scoring we have now because if time was passing 10x faster than normal, it probably wouldn't make sense to go into a room for 5 pieces of gold.)

#18364 1 2007-12-12T07:33:09Z Hendor
BLUR


When you look at a map, do you see places where a corner jump might save valuable seconds? Do you see enemies that you might just be able to squeeze past? Do you see doors that do not need to be opened? If you do, that's good. However, perhaps the best question is: Do you have the skills to play the way you see?

----------

I'll hold the dramatics here on in. As you may have guessed, this is a highscoring tournament. If you are interested, read on.

:: The teams
The first stage of this competition will be played in groups of 4; therefore, our total number of players must be a multiple of 4. Once we have enough people to play, I will put all the names into a hat and draw out the teams. Any players who get left out for one reason or another can be put onto a waiting list to jump in for someone if they have to drop out. I will not be playing.

If you are reading this after the competition has started, don't worry, you can still play. The only catch is that you have to play in a late-joiners thread. The only way players in the late-joiners group may advance is through the wildcards (see below).

:: The format
Competing within your randomly selected group, you will play three maps. Everyone in every group plays the same map at the same time. Each group does not have their own map. Based on your performance, you will be given points. The fastest player in each group will get most points, while the slowest person will get least. After playing through the three maps, the two players in each group with the most points will advance to the next stage. If two players are tied, each player's total time (in seconds) for all three maps is summed. The highest time wins the tiebreak. In addition to these 6 players advancing through the groups, 2 more people will advance via wild-cards. The remaining players who have the highest total time get these wild-cards. I'll get more into the format of the next stage when it gets closer to that time. For now, just know that submitting fast demos and getting lots of points is good.

:: The Scoring
  • 1st in group - 4 points
  • 2nd in group - 3 points
  • 3rd in group - 2 points
  • 4th in group - 1 point
To get points, you must submit a demo. If you do not submit a demo, you lose a point.

:: Bonus Points
Too boring so far? Yep, I thought so.
  • Making the Cut - Whenever I assign a map, I will also assign a certain time that I feel would be quite challenging for most players to reach. Every player who breaks this time gets +1 point.
  • The Golden Player - The single player who submits the fastest time out of everyone will get + 1 point. This player will likely accumulate 6 points on the round (4 + 1 + 1).
:: Rules
-You will always have a minimum of one week to complete your demo. This should be way more than enough.
-Complete your demo in Unreality's program, which you can find here. See below for instructions.
-The maps will be chosen from NUMA. Please do not post the demo you are submitting on the map's page before the contest is over.
-Do not PM me your demo.
-Discuss the map and your experiences here as much as you want. You may post your time (or a fake time) if you wish.

:: About NReality

I will resubmit all maps used in this competition to NUMA in order to enable a "privatecontest" feature that lets only the map creator(me) see the submitted demos. So, you may submit as many demos as you want, whenever you want.

1 - Download the program using the above link and unzip the contents to a folder somewhere on your computer.
2 - Open the program from this folder. If you move the program out of the folder, you will lose some features.
3 - Click "userlevels" from the main menu.
4 - Enter the map number as given in the NUMA web address.
5 - Press "Load Map" and the program will automatically download the map from NUMA. Neat, eh.
6 - Click on the downloaded map just as in the old userlevels to play it.
7 - Any personal best will be recorded to the right of the map title.
8 - To submit your demo, ctrl-click it. (command-click it for Macs.)
9 - You will only see your own demo in the list. Everyone else's is hidden from you.

#389548 2 2007-12-15T05:19:40Z Hendor
When playing episodes, you start the first level with 90 seconds. This is all the time you get for all 5 levels. This is rarely enough to complete them all, so each piece of gold you get adds 2 seconds to your time. Episode 59 does not have very much gold. The gold that it does have is also quite difficult and time consuming to get. So, I'd advise just getting through the first 4 levels as quick as you can and then using the gold at the beginning of level 5 to bolster your time. If you want to see the advanced routes that get all of the gold, check out the level and episode highscores.

yeah freaking right. the radiation comes directly from the sun. it is a huge stream of charged particles, which is strong enough to blow atmospheres away.

Quote: Bunniesandsheep, 2007-12-17T02:18:28Z
it consists of charged particles, similar to those of a nuclear explosion, which travel at 240,000 kph.
Yes, they are similar to the particles emitted by a nuclear explosion because that's exactly what the sun is. And 240,000 km/h? That's miles off (many miles actually.) The speed of all electromagnetic radiation is 3 x 10^8 m/s or just over 1 billion km/h.

Quote: Bunniesandsheep, 2007-12-17T02:18:28Z
you are saying that you can stand in the middle of a nuclear explosion, and survive, and last for a year before you get radiation poisoning.
No one is saying that you can stand in the middle of a nuclear explosion and survive. We're saying that you can be 1.49610^11 m (149,600,000,000 m) away and survive. In the 8 minutes it takes radiation from the sun to reach earth, the individual particles have spread out immensely.

The longest a human has spent in space is 90 days. [1] They only took in 7.74 rad of radiation. You are only getting lethal levels when you approach 400 rad. [2] That's equivalent to 4651 days in space, or roughly 13 years.

Note I've documented my sources. Where are yours?

#390815 20 2007-12-18T23:29:22Z Hendor
Another double post...

I've been thinking about what will happen after the first three rounds and I've realized that having just six people advance will limit the number of people will can be included in all this fun. Currently, we would have four people playing two 1v1 elimination rounds and the two top players having a "bye". The winners of the 1v1 matches would meet the byes and play semi-finals. I don't like this.

To maximize the number of players in the post-group stages without increasing the number of rounds, we would need 8 players (four 1v1s). Also, I've just received a request from a player to join the competition. To accommodate both of these, I've introduced two "wild-card" spots and an additional "late-joiners" group.

The top two players in each of the three original groups still advance, just like before. In addition, the two remaining players who have played every map with the lowest total time will advance. Finally, anyone in the additional "late-joiners" group may only advance via the "wild-card" method. So, to everyone in a group right now, all that's changed is you have a way to advance if you're not in the top 2. To anyone not registered who wants to be in the late-joiners group, post here before 1 PM on Dec. 25 saying you want to join and I'll put you in.

Oh, say hello to the first late-joiner, Infernus.

#391209 44 2007-12-19T22:53:57Z Hendor
Quote: Infernus, December 19, 2007 04:57 pm
Quote: Hendor, December 18, 2007 11:29 pm
In addition, the two remaining players who have played every map with the lowest total time will advance.
Shouldn't it be the highest total score?
No. The people in the late-joiners group will not be getting points. This might seem unfair, but it makes sense; I'll try to explain. Sorry I neglected to inform you of this earlier. I hadn't thought about it much.

The top two finishers in the three groups will be likely the ones who get 3 and 4 points on each map. Therefore, the people who do not advance would be the ones who were getting 1 and 2 points. If I were to give the two people in the late-joiners category 4 points for 1st place and 3 points for 2nd place, you two would be the wildcards without a doubt if it was decided through points. It would be like a free pass to the second round. So, to clarify, the wildcard positions are only available to those who play all three maps and they are decided through the lowest total time. I hope that makes sense.

EDIT: I realize that not getting points kinda takes the fun of the bonus points out of the game for you two. If anyone can think of a way to incorporate them, please make a suggestion.

#394526 144 2007-12-29T20:55:53Z Hendor
Alright! It works excellently. So, here's the map plus instructions:

The map: Evilghost: The Haunt by Maximum Ride
The time to beat: 119.250 (I hope this is more of a challenge)
Due Date: Sunday, January 6, 2008 at 8:00 PM (gives you just over 8 days)

How to work Unreality's program:

1- Download the latest edition of NReality from his thread.
2- Unzip contents to a folder on desktop or wherever you want.
3- Open the program from this folder and go into the userlevels menu.
4- Look at my attached picture and enter "112440" in the same box I did.
5- Click "Load Map" and the program will download the map data for you.
6- Click the loaded map to play it.
7- Any personal best will be displayed to the right of the map.
8- You can submit this personal best at any point by ctrl-clicking the time.

If you have any questions, ask away. Enjoy!

#395592 15 2008-01-02T01:25:59Z Hendor
Quote: Sniperwhere, January 01, 2008 07:33 pm
Sounds interesting. But one thing I'm confused about is the scoring. You said that if you get a better time after the set ending time, that it counts as no demo posted. What do you mean by that?
He's using this rule because we're using Unreality's program, which incorporates highscoring and NUMA (It's very cool if haven't seen it yet.) Basically, you load a map from NUMA into the program using the map number. When it's loaded, a highscore board also appears that displays that top times submitted for this map. Whenever a run is submitted, it is also stamped with the time (of day) at which the person submitted it. All he is saying is that if you submit a new personal best after the deadline, it won't count, cause there's no way of knowing what your time was before.

#398349 241 2008-01-07T16:14:25Z Hendor
Quote: ska, January 07, 2008 09:42 am

You didn't have to.

Quote: ska, 2008-01-07T14:42:27Z
Excuse my blantant inability to read but I'm confused, are the only players who can be eliminated the late-joiners and where did all this wild-card shit come from? 
From the rules:
"After playing through three maps, the two players in each group with the most points will advance to the next stage. If two players are tied, each player's total time (in seconds) for all three maps is summed. The highest time wins the tiebreak. In addition to these 6 players advancing through the groups, 2 more people will advance via wild-cards. These wildcards are the remaining two players who have the highest total time. Late-joining players may ONLY advance through one of the wildcard spots."

I introduced them for the late-joiners and to accomodate the random teams. Having them ensures that everyone who should advance does. If, say, the three best players in the competition were randomly put into the same group and there were no wildcards, one of them wouldn't get to go through. With the wildcard, however, he would. Most of the major North American sports leagues have wildcards for the same reason.

#405787 9 2008-01-31T07:33:13Z Hendor
Oh, just thought I'd mention that you can completely disregard the whole 163 dpi thing as that means absolutely nothing unless you're printing an image. Simply make a 320 x 480 image and save it as a PNG as I did above. Harley, here's where you were confused:

The tech specs for the iPhone and iPod Touch say the resolutions are 320 x 480 @ 163 dpi. What this means is that the screen is 320 x 480 pixels, just like your monitor may be 1024 x 768. The 163 dpi simply refers to the distance between pixels. In one square inch of an iPhone screen there are 163 x 163 (26569) pixels. Similarily, your computer monitor is 72 dpi. One inch of the monitor has 72 x 72 (5184) pixels. You never have to take into account the dpi if you're only working with images on screens. If we were going to print these images for some reason, to achieve a resolution comparable to the iPhone, we woud need a image with a dpi of 163.

#414872 4 2008-03-08T05:38:38Z Hendor
Here is everything you could possibly want to know about screenshotting on a Mac.

Command+Shift+3 = Capture entire screen and save as a file
Command+Control+Shift+3 = Capture entire screen and copy to the clipboard
Command+Shift+4 = Capture dragged rectangular area and save as a file
Command+Control+Shift+4 = Capture dragged area and copy to the clipboard
Command+Shift+4 then Space bar = Capture a window, menu, desktop icon, or the menu bar and save as a file
Command+Control+Shift+4 then Space bar = Capture a window, menu, desktop icon, or the menu bar and copy to the clipboard

#425802 11 2008-04-06T22:07:39Z Hendor
I read something in the newspaper a while back that said kids who play video-games are better at spatially numeric tasks, like looking at a bunch of shapes (or counter-terrorists) and telling you how many there are without counting. Not sure if there is a real world application for this, but it's interesting.

Also, to agree with LV, I don't think playing games willl have any effect on your hand/eye coordination. You are using completely different systems. Simply pressing buttons in relation to something that happens on-screen should not train yourself to do anything better in your life besides pressing buttons. Getting better at catching a ball (or even shooting a gun) happens when the muscles used to catch a ball or shoot a gun build a memory of what they had to do to succesfully complete their task. This wouldn't happen if you were catching a ball while playing Madden '08 or shooting a gun playing CS. The only thing playing these games will imporove is your game-playing abilities.

#426085 13 2008-04-07T22:56:50Z Hendor
Quote: Atilla, April 06, 2008 08:47 pm
@Hendor: Incorrect. I recall a study showing that surgeons who play Halo before an operation actually perform the surgery faster and with greater accuracy. Also, the military is known to use games for training purposes. Even if they don't make you an expert marksman, they can certainly teach you strategy.
I wasn't talking about strategy at all, just hand-eye coordination. Everything I wrote was basically taken out of the notes to my psychomotor behavior class which is taught by one of the top researchers in the field of motor learning. We actually covered the specific study you were referring to about the surgeons and I nearly included stuff about it last post. The "study" was extremely informal and the surgery component was entirely simulated (much like a video game.) It's still not clear whether better performance in the simulated surgery they used translates into better performance in surgery on a live patient. The executive director of the Australian College of Surgeons even said about this study: "What it shows at the moment is only that if you repeatedly play video games you get better at playing video games."(1) Exactly what I said.

Whatever, this is way too nitty gritty for these forums. All I was saying is that if you want to get better at shooting a gun, don't expect playing Halo 10 hours a day to do this.

#434725 5 2008-05-09T07:13:13Z Hendor
Don't be confused by that post PaulaWKY made. It seemed she was trying to make you believe that cooking your vegetables was better than eating them raw, which is blatantly wrong. Look at the water next time you boil carrots and it'll be orange, loaded with carotene, vitamin A and other vitamins/minerals that leaked out of the carrot as it cooked. What the article she based that on said was that cooking foods can increase the digestive uptake of some specific nutrients in some specific foods. Unless your goal is to consume these specific nutrients, you're best off nutritionally going with eating your veggies raw.

Here's a good guidline for preparing food with minimal nutrient loss.

Quote: ToeFacekiller, May 15, 2008 11:02 pm
That's great and all, but how's it going to work with 32 people?

I had been under the impression that each pairing was getting their own map.

Also, about the seeding/no seeding. If there is no seeding, the best two players could play each other in the first round. This isn't really fair, which is why most all professional sporting events use this. Best example for our purposes is tennis, where the 128 players are arranged so that the top two seeds couldn't possibly meet until the finals. Also, no one in the top four could meet before the semifinals and so on...

#455732 7 2008-07-29T05:17:46Z Hendor
Yay, a puzzle.

<solve>

IBF stands for Instant Backup File. I believe it's the format that forumer uses to store typed data en route to further storage. See the following code for the Username and Password boxes:

User Name: <input type="text" class="forminput" size="10" name="UserName" onfocus="this.value=''" value="{ibf.lang.qli_name}" />
Password: <input type='password' class='forminput' size='10' name='PassWord' onfocus="this.value=''" value='ibfrules' />

As you can see, each line begins with the text "<input", which starts a field where the user can enter data. You can probably figure out nearly everything else except the "value=" right near the end of each line. It is an attribute, modifying the <input> tag. In this case, it is indicating the default value of the field. The default value here would be nothing, a blank box. This is also what you would want the field to show after you submit your password. Rather than simply state this in the code, I believe forumer is referring to a stylesheet titled "ipfrules" pertaining to the rules that should be followed when using an Instant Backup File. I'm sure Little Viking could help us out here.

</solve>

Sorry to disrupt your fanciful thoughts of a bored programmer.

Typeracer

It's a MOTR! You click Join a Race and within seconds you're in a race with a minimum of one other person (most I've seen is 7.) You each control a little car, which moves across the screen according to how far along you are in typing a paragraph of text. The person who types it all first wins the race. The site keeps track of your average WPM and your record and all that stuff. There's also an option to play a friend and I want to try it.

To play against me, visit http://play.typeracer.com/index?room=dHI6aGVuZG9y at the same time as I'm there to enter a little race/chat room. Get your own room from the main menu. Go to IRC to plan the game

#464675 7 2008-08-26T23:58:24Z Hendor
Quote: blue_tetris, August 26, 2008 01:19 pm
A rather depressing thing to listen to. There's a point where the notes you are tracking with your ear become so low that you move to track higher notes, and it sounds like a "jump" in your mind's ear--so to speak. In that collegehumor video, they end the recording at about that moment so it sounds like a smooth transition when you restart it yourself, I think.
I think that's the idea. First time I heard it, I figured they had to be using a scale on two different octaves, slowly fading from the high one to the low one over the course of the octave. For example, if the first note of their scale was a C, the next note would be 6/7th, or 86% of the D and 1/7th, or 14% of the D that is one octave lower. By the time you get to B in the scale, you are only playing 14% of the B and 86% of the one in the lower octave. It should sound perfectly natural to move from that to the original C. Because you go through the entire scale and end up where you started, you can reason that you couldn't have actually gone anywhere at all, and if you think about it, you didn't. If you think of your base C as 0 and each note representing one number, increasing and decreasing, each pair of notes "averages" back to 0 (ex. (6/7 * 1) + (1/7 *(-6)) / 2=0, or C) Of course, the combination of Ds doesn't sound like a C, music doesn't works that way.

Hope that made sense.

The internet is full of abbreviations that we use all of the time, yet rarely say out loud to other people. Here's my list; add or remove anything if you want.

JPG - "jay-pegg"

GIF - "jiff" (it really should be "gif" with a hard g because the g stand for graphics.)

PNG - "p.n.g."

ZIP - "zip"

RAR - "rar"

ISO - "ice-oh"

AVI - "a.v.i."

DivX - "div-ex" (not "divicks", which is also pretty popular.)

EXE - "ex" (a lot of people say "exey", which surprised me. I say "ex", but think executable in my head.)

MPG - "em-peg"

TXT - "text"

DOC - "dawk"

WAV - "wave"

OS X - "oh-ess-ex" (nearly everyone else in the world says "oh-ess-ten")

WMA/WMV - "windows-media-audio/video" (I don't pronounce the abbreviation at all.)

Torrent - "you-torrent" (should be "mew-torrent" I guess.)

nVidia - "en-vid-ee-ah"

DMG - "damage"


There are probably more, but I can't think of them at the moment.

#471944 12 2008-09-20T21:20:51Z Hendor
Quote: T3chno, September 19, 2008 10:39 pm
Was it AB that was the universal donor and O the universal acceptor?

No, it's the other way around. Read on.


Your blood type refers to which type of antigens and antibodies are found in the blood. An antigen is simply an identifier, while it is an antibody's job to attack certain things it believes to be harmful. People with blood type A have A antigens and anti-B antibodies. If they are given blood from someone with blood type B, their anti-B antibodies will reject it. Basically what this means is that you can't get blood that has antibodies against your blood type. So... people with the blood type AB can get blood from anyone because their blood has both A and B antigens and NO antibodies, or else their blood would attack itself. Because it has no antibodies, it is impossible for it to attack anything. Conversely, type O is the universal donor, because it has NO antigens and both A and B antibodies. Therefore, no blood type will attack it, because it simply can't identify it.

A common question after hearing this is "Why don't the anti-A antibodies in type O blood attack type A blood when the type O is transfused into type A." This question makes sense because type A blood has A antigens, so why don't the anti-A antibodies in the O blood attack them? The answer is that blood antibodies are found in the blood plasma, as opposed to the antigens, which are on the surface of the red blood cells. The plasma is not included in a transfusion.

Sorry for the long winded answer, but I've just recently learned this and was testing myself. I'm type A, don't know the Rh factor.

Ad - You will have received an O allele from your mom. Your type depends 100% on what allele your dad gave you. If he gave an A allele, you're AO, which is type A. If he gave a B, you're BO, which is type B. If he gave an O, you're OO, which is type O. The only thing you can't be is AB. Also, seeing as you don't know your dad's blood type, you have basically a 33% chance for the above three types.
